My aunt was so embarrassed, she kept trying to hide it under the pews, but he would just dive under there and get it. The pews were that really dark oak, you know? They smelled like lemon polish and old paper. The hymnals always had those super thin pages that would rip if you turned them too fast... \n\nBut wait, what were you saying? About voices? Yeah, no, just the radio stuff in the truck. Nothing else.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a head injury, seizure, or other medical problem affecting your brain?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think so. I mean, I've never had a seizure or anything like that. I did hit my head once when I was little, but it wasn't a big deal. I fell off the monkey bars at the park near my house. It was one of those old metal playgrounds, you know, where the slide gets super hot in the summer? I burned the back of my legs on it once, wearing these little denim shorts my mom got from Kmart. They had a little embroidered daisy on the pocket. \n\nMy mom loved daisies. She actually tried to grow them in our front yard, but the soil was too clay-like or something, so they never really took off. She ended up putting in these big plastic flamingos instead. The neighbor, Mr. Henderson, hated them. He was always out there with his lawnmower, perfectly edging his grass. He had this vintage push mower that didn't even use gas, just the blades spinning around. It made this really satisfying *shhh-shhh* sound when he pushed it. He used to sharpen the blades in his garage, and you could see the sparks flying out if you walked by at night... \n\nBut, um, anyway. No, no brain problems or anything like that. Just that one time on the monkey bars, but I just got a little bump, I didn't even have to go to the doctor for it.\n\nDoctor: How long have these tracking and radio-comment experiences been going on?\n\nPatient: Um, well, the tracking stuff through the computer and the radio comments... it's been really clear to me for about the past two months. It started right around the time the leaves started changing color. I love autumn, honestly. The trees along the interstate get so vibrant, especially up near the state line. There's this one stretch of highway where it's just a tunnel of bright orange and red maples. \n\nMy grandmother used to collect maple leaves and press them in these heavy old encyclopedias she had in her den. They were the Encyclopedia Britannica, the ones with the gold lettering on the spine. She had the whole set, from A to Z, lined up on this dark mahogany bookshelf. The shelf always smelled like cedar and old dust. I tried to read the 'D' volume once, for a school project on dinosaurs, but the pictures were all black and white and looked really outdated. The T-Rex looked like a giant kangaroo standing straight up. \n\nI actually saw a kangaroo once at this little petting zoo they set up in a mall parking lot when I was a teenager. It was so weird, seeing it just standing there on the asphalt next to a Sears. The mall is closed down now, though. They boarded up the doors with this cheap plywood that got all warped from the rain... \n\nBut, um, yeah. About two months, I'd say. That's when I really started noticing the radio and the computer tracking.\n\nDoctor: Did these beliefs or experiences start suddenly after you were sick or otherwise medically unwell?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I wasn't sick or anything. I mean, I haven't really been sick since I had a really bad flu, like, maybe three years ago? I was stuck in my apartment for a week just drinking peppermint tea. I have this really great mug I use for tea, actually. It’s huge, almost like a soup bowl with a handle, and it has this little painted scene of a lighthouse on it. I got it at this gift shop in Maine when I was hauling a load of lumber up there. \n\nMaine is so beautiful, but the roads can get really narrow, especially the coastal routes. There was this one little diner I stopped at near Portland that had the most amazing blueberry pancakes. They served them with real maple syrup in these tiny glass pitchers that were always a little bit sticky on the handle. The waitress there had this crazy curly red hair, kind of like that actress from those old 80s movies, you know? My mom loved 80s movies. We used to rent VHS tapes from Blockbuster every Friday night when I was a kid. I remember the plastic cases always had that distinct smell, kind of like ozone and dusty plastic, and you always had to make sure you rewound them or they'd charge you that extra fee... \n\nWait, what were you asking me? Oh, if I was sick before the radio stuff started? Yeah, no, I was perfectly healthy. It didn't start from a cold or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself becoming much less able to think clearly or keep your thoughts organized than you used to?\n\nPatient: Um, well, my thoughts... I guess they do get kind of jumbled sometimes. Like at work, I'm a dispatcher, you know? And lately, I'll get stuck just rearranging the magnets and the route slips on the board. I have to put them in this very specific pattern, otherwise it feels like the whole board is going to go wrong and all the trucks will get lost. It's a big whiteboard, the kind that always has those faint ghost markers left behind from old dry erase pens. I hate the smell of dry erase markers, honestly. They smell like rubbing alcohol and cheap cherry candy. \n\nMy aunt used to keep a bowl of those hard cherry candies on her coffee table. They were wrapped in this crinkly cellophane that made so much noise when you tried to open them. She had this tiny little poodle named Fifi that would always bark whenever someone crinkled a wrapper. Fifi had these little bows in her hair, always pink or purple, and she wore this rhinestone collar that caught the light from the living room lamp. The lamp had a fringed shade, like something out of a 1920s speakeasy. I went to a 1920s themed party once, for New Year's Eve. Everyone was wearing flapper dresses and drinking out of those wide, shallow champagne glasses... \n\nBut, um, yeah, at work. I also find myself repeating the last few words a driver says over the radio before I answer them. People look at me like I'm being difficult or weird, but I'm just trying to keep everything straight. It's like I have to do it to keep things from falling apart.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you felt unusually depressed?\n\nPatient: Um, depressed? No, I don't think so. I mean, I don't feel unusually sad or anything. I actually really like my apartment, it gets a lot of good light in the mornings. I have this spider plant hanging in the window, the kind that shoots off those little baby plants? I've been trying to propagate them in these little glass yogurt jars. The yogurt was really good, actually, it was this French brand that comes in glass instead of plastic. I try to avoid plastic when I can, you know, for the ocean. \n\nI saw this documentary once about sea turtles getting caught in plastic rings. It was so sad. Sea turtles are amazing, they can navigate across the whole ocean just using the earth's magnetic field. Kind of like a built-in compass. My dad gave me a compass when I first got my driver's license, just in case I ever got lost. It was brass and really heavy, with a little latch on the side. I used to keep it in the glove compartment of my first car, which was this beat-up old Honda Civic. The heater in that car was broken, so I always had to drive with a blanket over my lap in the winter. \n\nWinter can be tough, with the snow and all, but I don't get depressed. So, no, I haven't really felt that way.\n\nDoctor: Have these tracking beliefs and radio comments been going on for at least one month?\n\nPatient: Um, a month? Let's see... yeah, it's been about two months now that it's been really clear to me. The tracking through the computer system and the comments over the radio, I mean. It started right around the time the company switched to that new dispatch software. The interface on it is awful, all these bright neon colors that just hurt your eyes after staring at the screen for eight hours. I had to buy these special blue-light blocking glasses just to deal with it. They have these thick black frames, kind of retro looking, like something Buddy Holly would wear. \n\nMy uncle actually met Buddy Holly once, way back in the day. He used to play the bass in this local rockabilly band and they opened for him at this little venue in Texas. My uncle still has the ticket stub framed in his hallway. He has a whole wall of framed stuff, mostly old concert posters and vinyl records. He has this one really rare pressing of a Beatles album, the one with the butcher cover? He keeps it in this special UV-protected glass case so it doesn't fade. I love the smell of old vinyl records, it's so dusty and warm. \n\nI used to go to this thrift store downtown just to flip through the crates of records. They had this huge orange tabby cat that just lived in the store and slept on the stacks of jeans... Wait, sorry, what was I saying? Oh, right, the tracking and the radio stuff. Yeah, about two months now.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your work or daily functioning getting worse since these experiences started?\n\nPatient: Um, getting worse? I guess... yeah, it has been getting worse. Like, at work, I've really fallen behind on my route reports. And my instructions to the drivers, they've gotten so confusing lately that they actually complain to management about me. It's just hard to focus with the new software and everything. Management is always so focused on efficiency, they put up these motivational posters in the breakroom. There's one with an eagle soaring over a mountain that says \"Excellence\" at the bottom. I've always wanted to see a bald eagle in the wild. We went camping once in the Pacific Northwest, my husband and I, hoping to see one. \n\nWe stayed in this little A-frame cabin near the Hoh Rainforest. It rained almost the whole time, but it was that really fine, misty rain that just makes everything look green and lush. We had to wear these bright yellow rain slickers whenever we went hiking. They were so stiff and made this loud swooshing sound every time you took a step. My husband slipped on a wet log and completely covered his jeans in mud. We had to wash them in the cabin's tiny little sink using this bar of lavender soap. Lavender is supposed to be relaxing, right? I used to buy these little sachets of dried lavender to keep in my dresser drawers to make my clothes smell nice. \n\nI don't really do that anymore, though. Actually, at home, things have been slipping too. My husband has had to take over making all the meals and handling phone calls and just... basic planning. I'm just not keeping up with it all anymore. It's like I don't have the energy or the focus to manage the house on top of everything else.\n\nDoctor: Have these beliefs or experiences been going on for six months or longer?\n\nPatient: Um, six months? Let me think... it's actually been longer than that, I think. This whole thing has been building for about eight months now. It started right around the time they put me in that temporary dispatch lead role at work. That was such a stressful time, they didn't even give me a pay bump for the extra responsibility. They just said it would look good on my resume. Resumes are so stressful to write, aren't they? Trying to fit your whole life onto one piece of paper. I used this really nice, heavy cardstock for mine, kind of an off-white color. Ivory, I think it was called. \n\nI bought a whole ream of it at this little stationery shop downtown. They had all these beautiful fountain pens in glass display cases. I've always wanted to learn calligraphy, the kind with the swooping letters. My grandmother had beautiful handwriting, she used to write me letters every week when I was in college. She always sealed the envelopes with wax, this deep red wax with a stamp of a rose pressed into it. She grew the most amazing roses in her backyard, these huge pink ones that smelled like perfume. She had this old wooden trellis that they climbed up, and she'd spend hours out there with her pruning shears. \n\nShe used to wear this big straw hat to keep the sun off her face. It had a wide brim and a green ribbon tied around it. I think I still have that hat somewhere in a box in my attic. The attic gets so hot in the summer, though, I try not to go up there if I don't have to... Wait, what was the question? Oh, right, the six months thing. Yeah, it's been about eight months, since that temporary lead role.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any period when you were much more talkative or felt like you had racing thoughts?\n\nPatient: More talkative? No, I don't think so. I mean, I'm usually pretty quiet, actually. Not like my cousin Sarah, she can talk on the phone for literally hours. She used to rack up these huge phone bills back when you actually had to pay for long-distance minutes. My aunt would get so mad at her. They had this old rotary phone in their kitchen, it was this awful mustard yellow color with a really long, coiled cord that was always getting tangled up in knots. You could stretch it all the way into the living room if you wanted to sit on the couch while you talked. \n\nThey had this really itchy plaid couch, mostly brown and orange. It matched the shag carpet they had in there. I always hated walking on that carpet barefoot because you never knew what was hiding in it, like dropped paperclips or something. I stepped on a thumbtack once when I was little and it hurt so bad. My mom had to put a bandage on it with little Snoopy cartoons all over it. Snoopy was my favorite, I had a stuffed animal of him that I carried everywhere. His ears were made of this really soft black velvet material. Velvet always collects so much dust and lint, though, you have to use those sticky rollers on it constantly. I buy the lint rollers in bulk from the warehouse club because we go through so many... \n\nWait, sorry, what did you ask me? Racing thoughts? No, my thoughts don't really race, I don't think I get like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing these radio comments and feeling tracked continuously for at least six months without a period of feeling completely back to normal?\n\nPatient: Six months? Um, no, not the radio stuff. The tracking through the computer system and those radio comments, that's only felt really clear to me for about the past two months. It was right around the time they updated our dispatch software and I had to get a new mousepad for my desk because my old one was peeling. I ordered one online that has a picture of a sea turtle on it. Sea turtles are amazing, they can live for like a hundred years. I watched this documentary about them on PBS where the little babies hatch on the beach and have to scramble to the ocean before the seagulls get them. \n\nSeagulls are so aggressive, aren't they? One stole a french fry right out of my hand at the boardwalk a few years ago. It just swooped down out of nowhere. I dropped my whole tray of fries on the wooden planks. The fries were covered in Old Bay seasoning, which is honestly the best way to eat them. You can put Old Bay on anything, really. I tried it on popcorn once, but it made me cough because the powder gets caught in the back of your throat. Coughing fits are the worst, especially in a quiet place like a movie theater. I always bring a bottle of water with me just in case. The crinkly plastic bottles are so loud though, you have to wait for a loud scene in the movie to take a drink so you don't bother anyone. I hate bothering people... \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, the six months. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Before two months ago? Um, no, I don't think so. The radio comments and the tracking stuff, that's all pretty new. Before that, it was mostly just the stress from work, you know, feeling like everyone was watching me but not in the same way. It was more like... micromanaging. My old manager, Susan, she was the queen of micromanaging. She used to stand right behind my chair while I was typing up the logs. I could smell her perfume, it was this really strong, powdery floral scent, kind of like lilies. I've never really liked lilies, they always remind me of funerals. 

My great-aunt's funeral had so many lilies, the whole church smelled like them. It was a really old church with these beautiful stained glass windows. One of the windows had a picture of a dove with an olive branch. Doves are supposed to be a symbol of peace, right? I saw a white dove in the park once, just sitting on a park bench like a person. It was eating a piece of a soft pretzel someone had dropped. Soft pretzels are so good, especially the ones from that little cart near the subway station. They always put way too much salt on them, though, so you have to brush most of it off before you eat it. Otherwise, you get so thirsty, and the water fountains in the subway are always broken or they look too dirty to use. I carry hand sanitizer everywhere just in case I have to touch anything down there. It has a little silicone holder shaped like a penguin that clips onto my bag. Penguins are so cute when they waddle... 

Oh, sorry, what was I saying? The tracking before two months ago? No, I never had anything like that before. Just the last two months.

It’s a really weird building, honestly, it looks like an old converted bank. They still have the drive-thru window with that thick bulletproof glass, but they just use it for storage now. I saw they had boxes of printer paper stacked all the way to the ceiling in there. \n\nIt reminded me of my first job out of high school, working at this local print shop. We used to do all the flyers for the county fair. The fair was always so loud, but they had this one booth that sold deep-fried Oreos. Have you ever had one of those? They get all gooey on the inside and the batter is super crispy... I tried making them at home once in my little Fry Daddy, but the oil got way too hot and smoked up my whole apartment. The smoke detector wouldn't stop beeping for like an hour, I had to open all the windows and it was freezing outside. \n\nBut, um, yeah. No drugs or anything like that for me. Just regular stuff, like ibuprofen if my back hurts from driving.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices that other people could not hear?\n\nPatient: Um, well, no, not like that. I mean, I hear the dispatch radio, like I said, and the people commenting on what I'm doing, but I guess anyone could hear that if they were in the cab with me. It's just coming through the speakers. I actually had to get my speakers replaced last year because the left side was crackling. I took it to this mechanic out in Gary, Indiana. He had this massive garage with, like, five bays, and there was this old vintage gas pump right in the middle of the waiting area. It was painted bright red, like a fire engine. \n\nMy cousin used to want to be a firefighter when we were kids. We had these little plastic helmets we got from a school assembly, and he wore his everywhere, even to church. My aunt was so embarrassed, she kept trying to hide it under the pews, but he would just dive under there and get it. The pews were that really dark oak, you know? They smelled like lemon polish and old paper. The hymnals always had those super thin pages that would rip if you turned them too fast... \n\nBut wait, what were you saying? About voices? Yeah, no, just the radio stuff in the truck. Nothing else.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a head injury, seizure, or other medical problem affecting your brain?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think so. I mean, I've never had a seizure or anything like that. I did hit my head once when I was little, but it wasn't a big deal. I fell off the monkey bars at the park near my house. It was one of those old metal playgrounds, you know, where the slide gets super hot in the summer? I burned the back of my legs on it once, wearing these little denim shorts my mom got from Kmart. They had a little embroidered daisy on the pocket. \n\nMy mom loved daisies. She actually tried to grow them in our front yard, but the soil was too clay-like or something, so they never really took off. She ended up putting in these big plastic flamingos instead. The neighbor, Mr. Henderson, hated them. He was always out there with his lawnmower, perfectly edging his grass. He had this vintage push mower that didn't even use gas, just the blades spinning around. It made this really satisfying *shhh-shhh* sound when he pushed it. He used to sharpen the blades in his garage, and you could see the sparks flying out if you walked by at night... \n\nBut, um, anyway. No, no brain problems or anything like that. Just that one time on the monkey bars, but I just got a little bump, I didn't even have to go to the doctor for it.\n\nDoctor: How long have these tracking and radio-comment experiences been going on?\n\nPatient: Um, well, the tracking stuff through the computer and the radio comments... it's been really clear to me for about the past two months. It started right around the time the leaves started changing color. I love autumn, honestly. The trees along the interstate get so vibrant, especially up near the state line. There's this one stretch of highway where it's just a tunnel of bright orange and red maples. \n\nMy grandmother used to collect maple leaves and press them in these heavy old encyclopedias she had in her den. They were the Encyclopedia Britannica, the ones with the gold lettering on the spine. She had the whole set, from A to Z, lined up on this dark mahogany bookshelf. The shelf always smelled like cedar and old dust. I tried to read the 'D' volume once, for a school project on dinosaurs, but the pictures were all black and white and looked really outdated. The T-Rex looked like a giant kangaroo standing straight up. \n\nI actually saw a kangaroo once at this little petting zoo they set up in a mall parking lot when I was a teenager. It was so weird, seeing it just standing there on the asphalt next to a Sears. The mall is closed down now, though. They boarded up the doors with this cheap plywood that got all warped from the rain... \n\nBut, um, yeah. About two months, I'd say. That's when I really started noticing the radio and the computer tracking.\n\nDoctor: Did these beliefs or experiences start suddenly after you were sick or otherwise medically unwell?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I wasn't sick or anything. I mean, I haven't really been sick since I had a really bad flu, like, maybe three years ago? I was stuck in my apartment for a week just drinking peppermint tea. I have this really great mug I use for tea, actually. It’s huge, almost like a soup bowl with a handle, and it has this little painted scene of a lighthouse on it. I got it at this gift shop in Maine when I was hauling a load of lumber up there. \n\nMaine is so beautiful, but the roads can get really narrow, especially the coastal routes. There was this one little diner I stopped at near Portland that had the most amazing blueberry pancakes. They served them with real maple syrup in these tiny glass pitchers that were always a little bit sticky on the handle. The waitress there had this crazy curly red hair, kind of like that actress from those old 80s movies, you know? My mom loved 80s movies. We used to rent VHS tapes from Blockbuster every Friday night when I was a kid. I remember the plastic cases always had that distinct smell, kind of like ozone and dusty plastic, and you always had to make sure you rewound them or they'd charge you that extra fee... \n\nWait, what were you asking me? Oh, if I was sick before the radio stuff started? Yeah, no, I was perfectly healthy. It didn't start from a cold or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself becoming much less able to think clearly or keep your thoughts organized than you used to?\n\nPatient: Um, well, my thoughts... I guess they do get kind of jumbled sometimes. Like at work, I'm a dispatcher, you know? And lately, I'll get stuck just rearranging the magnets and the route slips on the board. I have to put them in this very specific pattern, otherwise it feels like the whole board is going to go wrong and all the trucks will get lost. It's a big whiteboard, the kind that always has those faint ghost markers left behind from old dry erase pens. I hate the smell of dry erase markers, honestly. They smell like rubbing alcohol and cheap cherry candy. \n\nMy aunt used to keep a bowl of those hard cherry candies on her coffee table. They were wrapped in this crinkly cellophane that made so much noise when you tried to open them. She had this tiny little poodle named Fifi that would always bark whenever someone crinkled a wrapper. Fifi had these little bows in her hair, always pink or purple, and she wore this rhinestone collar that caught the light from the living room lamp. The lamp had a fringed shade, like something out of a 1920s speakeasy. I went to a 1920s themed party once, for New Year's Eve. Everyone was wearing flapper dresses and drinking out of those wide, shallow champagne glasses... \n\nBut, um, yeah, at work. I also find myself repeating the last few words a driver says over the radio before I answer them. People look at me like I'm being difficult or weird, but I'm just trying to keep everything straight. It's like I have to do it to keep things from falling apart.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you felt unusually depressed?\n\nPatient: Um, depressed? No, I don't think so. I mean, I don't feel unusually sad or anything. I actually really like my apartment, it gets a lot of good light in the mornings. I have this spider plant hanging in the window, the kind that shoots off those little baby plants? I've been trying to propagate them in these little glass yogurt jars. The yogurt was really good, actually, it was this French brand that comes in glass instead of plastic. Sea turtles are amazing, they can navigate across the whole ocean just using the earth's magnetic field. Kind of like a built-in compass. My dad gave me a compass when I first got my driver's license, just in case I ever got lost. It was brass and really heavy, with a little latch on the side. I used to keep it in the glove compartment of my first car, which was this beat-up old Honda Civic. The heater in that car was broken, so I always had to drive with a blanket over my lap in the winter. \n\nWinter can be tough, with the snow and all, but I don't get depressed. Let's see... yeah, it's been about two months now that it's been really clear to me. The tracking through the computer system and the comments over the radio, I mean. The interface on it is awful, all these bright neon colors that just hurt your eyes after staring at the screen for eight hours. I had to buy these special blue-light blocking glasses just to deal with it. He used to play the bass in this local rockabilly band and they opened for him at this little venue in Texas. My uncle still has the ticket stub framed in his hallway. He has a whole wall of framed stuff, mostly old concert posters and vinyl records. He has this one really rare pressing of a Beatles album, the one with the butcher cover? He keeps it in this special UV-protected glass case so it doesn't fade. Yeah, about two months now.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your work or daily functioning getting worse since these experiences started?\n\nPatient: Um, getting worse? I guess... yeah, it has been getting worse. Like, at work, I've really fallen behind on my route reports. And my instructions to the drivers, they've gotten so confusing lately that they actually complain to management about me. It's just hard to focus with the new software and everything. Management is always so focused on efficiency, they put up these motivational posters in the breakroom. There's one with an eagle soaring over a mountain that says \"Excellence\" at the bottom. I've always wanted to see a bald eagle in the wild. We went camping once in the Pacific Northwest, my husband and I, hoping to see one. \n\nWe stayed in this little A-frame cabin near the Hoh Rainforest. It rained almost the whole time, but it was that really fine, misty rain that just makes everything look green and lush. We had to wear these bright yellow rain slickers whenever we went hiking. They were so stiff and made this loud swooshing sound every time you took a step. My husband slipped on a wet log and completely covered his jeans in mud. We had to wash them in the cabin's tiny little sink using this bar of lavender soap. Lavender is supposed to be relaxing, right? I used to buy these little sachets of dried lavender to keep in my dresser drawers to make my clothes smell nice. \n\nI don't really do that anymore, though. Actually, at home, things have been slipping too. My husband has had to take over making all the meals and handling phone calls and just... basic planning. I'm just not keeping up with it all anymore. They just said it would look good on my resume. Resumes are so stressful to write, aren't they? Trying to fit your whole life onto one piece of paper. They had all these beautiful fountain pens in glass display cases. I've always wanted to learn calligraphy, the kind with the swooping letters. My grandmother had beautiful handwriting, she used to write me letters every week when I was in college. She always sealed the envelopes with wax, this deep red wax with a stamp of a rose pressed into it. She grew the most amazing roses in her backyard, these huge pink ones that smelled like perfume. She had this old wooden trellis that they climbed up, and she'd spend hours out there with her pruning shears. \n\nShe used to wear this big straw hat to keep the sun off her face. It had a wide brim and a green ribbon tied around it. I think I still have that hat somewhere in a box in my attic. The attic gets so hot in the summer, though, I try not to go up there if I don't have to... Wait, what was the question? Oh, right, the six months thing. Yeah, it's been about eight months, since that temporary lead role.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any period when you were much more talkative or felt like you had racing thoughts?\n\nPatient: More talkative? No, I don't think so. I mean, I'm usually pretty quiet, actually. Not like my cousin Sarah, she can talk on the phone for literally hours. She used to rack up these huge phone bills back when you actually had to pay for long-distance minutes. My aunt would get so mad at her. They had this old rotary phone in their kitchen, it was this awful mustard yellow color with a really long, coiled cord that was always getting tangled up in knots. You could stretch it all the way into the living room if you wanted to sit on the couch while you talked. \n\nThey had this really itchy plaid couch, mostly brown and orange. It matched the shag carpet they had in there. I always hated walking on that carpet barefoot because you never knew what was hiding in it, like dropped paperclips or something. I stepped on a thumbtack once when I was little and it hurt so bad. My mom had to put a bandage on it with little Snoopy cartoons all over it. Snoopy was my favorite, I had a stuffed animal of him that I carried everywhere. His ears were made of this really soft black velvet material. Velvet always collects so much dust and lint, though, you have to use those sticky rollers on it constantly. I buy the lint rollers in bulk from the warehouse club because we go through so many... \n\nWait, sorry, what did you ask me? Racing thoughts? No, my thoughts don't really race, I don't think I get like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing these radio comments and feeling tracked continuously for at least six months without a period of feeling completely back to normal?\n\nPatient: Six months? I ordered one online that has a picture of a sea turtle on it. Sea turtles are amazing, they can live for like a hundred years. I watched this documentary about them on PBS where the little babies hatch on the beach and have to scramble to the ocean before the seagulls get them. \n\nSeagulls are so aggressive, aren't they? One stole a french fry right out of my hand at the boardwalk a few years ago. It just swooped down out of nowhere. I dropped my whole tray of fries on the wooden planks. The fries were covered in Old Bay seasoning, which is honestly the best way to eat them. You can put Old Bay on anything, really. I tried it on popcorn once, but it made me cough because the powder gets caught in the back of your throat. Coughing fits are the worst, especially in a quiet place like a movie theater. I always bring a bottle of water with me just in case. The crinkly plastic bottles are so loud though, you have to wait for a loud scene in the movie to take a drink so you don't bother anyone. I hate bothering people... \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, the six months. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
An actual outside radio? I'm not really sure, honestly. It's hard to pinpoint exactly where it's coming from. Sometimes it just feels like it's around me, kind of like when someone leaves the radio on low in the dispatch breakroom. I actually really like the rain, though. I bought a white noise machine for my bedroom that has a rain setting, but honestly, it sounds more like frying bacon than actual rain. Cooking bacon is such a hassle, the grease splatters everywhere and you have to wipe down the whole stove afterward. I use those lemon-scented cleaning wipes for everything, they smell so fresh and you can just throw them away when you're done... 

Wait, what were we talking about? The radio? Yeah, I don't really know if it's a real radio or if other people hear it, it's just hard to tell.

He had this huge collection of vintage license plates in his garage... he would hang them all up by year and state. He gave me a 1972 one from Colorado once. It had these really cool white mountains on a green background. I used to keep it in my locker when I was taking my associate's classes. I had this really tiny locker on the second floor, right next to the water fountain, so it was always kind of crowded between periods... \n\nBut, um, yeah, I don't really get unusually high or angry or anything like that. I saw they had boxes of printer paper stacked all the way to the ceiling in there. \n\nIt reminded me of my first job out of high school, working at this local print shop. We used to do all the flyers for the county fair. The fair was always so loud, but they had this one booth that sold deep-fried Oreos. Have you ever had one of those? They get all gooey on the inside and the batter is super crispy... I tried making them at home once in my little Fry Daddy, but the oil got way too hot and smoked up my whole apartment. He had this massive garage with, like, five bays, and there was this old vintage gas pump right in the middle of the waiting area. It was painted bright red, like a fire engine. \n\nMy cousin used to want to be a firefighter when we were kids. Yeah, no, just the radio stuff in the truck. Nothing else.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a head injury, seizure, or other medical problem affecting your brain?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think so. I mean, I've never had a seizure or anything like that. I did hit my head once when I was little, but it wasn't a big deal. I fell off the monkey bars at the park near my house. It was one of those old metal playgrounds, you know, where the slide gets super hot in the summer? I burned the back of my legs on it once, wearing these little denim shorts my mom got from Kmart. They had a little embroidered daisy on the pocket. \n\nMy mom loved daisies. She actually tried to grow them in our front yard, but the soil was too clay-like or something, so they never really took off. She ended up putting in these big plastic flamingos instead. The neighbor, Mr. Henderson, hated them. He was always out there with his lawnmower, perfectly edging his grass. He had this vintage push mower that didn't even use gas, just the blades spinning around. It made this really satisfying *shhh-shhh* sound when he pushed it. He used to sharpen the blades in his garage, and you could see the sparks flying out if you walked by at night... \n\nBut, um, anyway. No, no brain problems or anything like that. It didn't start from a cold or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself becoming much less able to think clearly or keep your thoughts organized than you used to?\n\nPatient: Um, well, my thoughts... I guess they do get kind of jumbled sometimes. Like at work, I'm a dispatcher, you know? And lately, I'll get stuck just rearranging the magnets and the route slips on the board. I have to put them in this very specific pattern, otherwise it feels like the whole board is going to go wrong and all the trucks will get lost. It's a big whiteboard, the kind that always has those faint ghost markers left behind from old dry erase pens. I hate the smell of dry erase markers, honestly. They smell like rubbing alcohol and cheap cherry candy. \n\nMy aunt used to keep a bowl of those hard cherry candies on her coffee table. They were wrapped in this crinkly cellophane that made so much noise when you tried to open them. She had this tiny little poodle named Fifi that would always bark whenever someone crinkled a wrapper. Fifi had these little bows in her hair, always pink or purple, and she wore this rhinestone collar that caught the light from the living room lamp. The lamp had a fringed shade, like something out of a 1920s speakeasy. I went to a 1920s themed party once, for New Year's Eve. Everyone was wearing flapper dresses and drinking out of those wide, shallow champagne glasses... \n\nBut, um, yeah, at work. I also find myself repeating the last few words a driver says over the radio before I answer them. People look at me like I'm being difficult or weird, but I'm just trying to keep everything straight. It's like I have to do it to keep things from falling apart.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you felt unusually depressed?\n\nPatient: Um, depressed? No, I don't think so. I mean, I don't feel unusually sad or anything. I actually really like my apartment, it gets a lot of good light in the mornings. I have this spider plant hanging in the window, the kind that shoots off those little baby plants? I've been trying to propagate them in these little glass yogurt jars. The yogurt was really good, actually, it was this French brand that comes in glass instead of plastic. I try to avoid plastic when I can, you know, for the ocean. \n\nI saw this documentary once about sea turtles getting caught in plastic rings. It was so sad. Sea turtles are amazing, they can navigate across the whole ocean just using the earth's magnetic field. Kind of like a built-in compass. My dad gave me a compass when I first got my driver's license, just in case I ever got lost. It was brass and really heavy, with a little latch on the side. I used to keep it in the glove compartment of my first car, which was this beat-up old Honda Civic. The heater in that car was broken, so I always had to drive with a blanket over my lap in the winter. \n\nWinter can be tough, with the snow and all, but I don't get depressed. So, no, I haven't really felt that way.\n\nDoctor: Have these tracking beliefs and radio comments been going on for at least one month?\n\nPatient: Um, a month? Let's see... yeah, it's been about two months now that it's been really clear to me. The tracking through the computer system and the comments over the radio, I mean. It started right around the time the company switched to that new dispatch software. The interface on it is awful, all these bright neon colors that just hurt your eyes after staring at the screen for eight hours. I had to buy these special blue-light blocking glasses just to deal with it. They have these thick black frames, kind of retro looking, like something Buddy Holly would wear. \n\nMy uncle actually met Buddy Holly once, way back in the day. He used to play the bass in this local rockabilly band and they opened for him at this little venue in Texas. My uncle still has the ticket stub framed in his hallway. He has a whole wall of framed stuff, mostly old concert posters and vinyl records. He has this one really rare pressing of a Beatles album, the one with the butcher cover? He keeps it in this special UV-protected glass case so it doesn't fade. I love the smell of old vinyl records, it's so dusty and warm. \n\nI used to go to this thrift store downtown just to flip through the crates of records. They had this huge orange tabby cat that just lived in the store and slept on the stacks of jeans... Wait, sorry, what was I saying? Oh, right, the tracking and the radio stuff. Yeah, about two months now.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your work or daily functioning getting worse since these experiences started?\n\nPatient: Um, getting worse? I guess... yeah, it has been getting worse. Like, at work, I've really fallen behind on my route reports. And my instructions to the drivers, they've gotten so confusing lately that they actually complain to management about me. It's just hard to focus with the new software and everything. Management is always so focused on efficiency, they put up these motivational posters in the breakroom. There's one with an eagle soaring over a mountain that says \"Excellence\" at the bottom. I've always wanted to see a bald eagle in the wild. We went camping once in the Pacific Northwest, my husband and I, hoping to see one. \n\nWe stayed in this little A-frame cabin near the Hoh Rainforest. It rained almost the whole time, but it was that really fine, misty rain that just makes everything look green and lush. We had to wear these bright yellow rain slickers whenever we went hiking. They were so stiff and made this loud swooshing sound every time you took a step. My husband slipped on a wet log and completely covered his jeans in mud. We had to wash them in the cabin's tiny little sink using this bar of lavender soap. Lavender is supposed to be relaxing, right? I used to buy these little sachets of dried lavender to keep in my dresser drawers to make my clothes smell nice. \n\nI don't really do that anymore, though. Actually, at home, things have been slipping too. My husband has had to take over making all the meals and handling phone calls and just... basic planning. I'm just not keeping up with it all anymore. It's like I don't have the energy or the focus to manage the house on top of everything else.\n\nDoctor: Have these beliefs or experiences been going on for six months or longer?\n\nPatient: Um, six months? Let me think... it's actually been longer than that, I think. This whole thing has been building for about eight months now. It started right around the time they put me in that temporary dispatch lead role at work. That was such a stressful time, they didn't even give me a pay bump for the extra responsibility. They just said it would look good on my resume. Resumes are so stressful to write, aren't they? Trying to fit your whole life onto one piece of paper. I used this really nice, heavy cardstock for mine, kind of an off-white color. Ivory, I think it was called. \n\nI bought a whole ream of it at this little stationery shop downtown. They had all these beautiful fountain pens in glass display cases. I've always wanted to learn calligraphy, the kind with the swooping letters. My grandmother had beautiful handwriting, she used to write me letters every week when I was in college. She always sealed the envelopes with wax, this deep red wax with a stamp of a rose pressed into it. She grew the most amazing roses in her backyard, these huge pink ones that smelled like perfume. She had this old wooden trellis that they climbed up, and she'd spend hours out there with her pruning shears. \n\nShe used to wear this big straw hat to keep the sun off her face. It had a wide brim and a green ribbon tied around it. I think I still have that hat somewhere in a box in my attic. The attic gets so hot in the summer, though, I try not to go up there if I don't have to... Wait, what was the question? Oh, right, the six months thing. Yeah, it's been about eight months, since that temporary lead role.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any period when you were much more talkative or felt like you had racing thoughts?\n\nPatient: More talkative? No, I don't think so. I mean, I'm usually pretty quiet, actually. Not like my cousin Sarah, she can talk on the phone for literally hours. She used to rack up these huge phone bills back when you actually had to pay for long-distance minutes. My aunt would get so mad at her. They had this old rotary phone in their kitchen, it was this awful mustard yellow color with a really long, coiled cord that was always getting tangled up in knots. You could stretch it all the way into the living room if you wanted to sit on the couch while you talked. \n\nThey had this really itchy plaid couch, mostly brown and orange. It matched the shag carpet they had in there. I always hated walking on that carpet barefoot because you never knew what was hiding in it, like dropped paperclips or something. I stepped on a thumbtack once when I was little and it hurt so bad. My mom had to put a bandage on it with little Snoopy cartoons all over it. Snoopy was my favorite, I had a stuffed animal of him that I carried everywhere. His ears were made of this really soft black velvet material. Velvet always collects so much dust and lint, though, you have to use those sticky rollers on it constantly. I buy the lint rollers in bulk from the warehouse club because we go through so many... \n\nWait, sorry, what did you ask me? Racing thoughts? No, my thoughts don't really race, I don't think I get like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing these radio comments and feeling tracked continuously for at least six months without a period of feeling completely back to normal?\n\nPatient: Six months? Um, no, not the radio stuff. The tracking through the computer system and those radio comments, that's only felt really clear to me for about the past two months. It was right around the time they updated our dispatch software and I had to get a new mousepad for my desk because my old one was peeling. I ordered one online that has a picture of a sea turtle on it. Sea turtles are amazing, they can live for like a hundred years. I watched this documentary about them on PBS where the little babies hatch on the beach and have to scramble to the ocean before the seagulls get them. \n\nSeagulls are so aggressive, aren't they? One stole a french fry right out of my hand at the boardwalk a few years ago. It just swooped down out of nowhere. I dropped my whole tray of fries on the wooden planks. The fries were covered in Old Bay seasoning, which is honestly the best way to eat them. You can put Old Bay on anything, really. I tried it on popcorn once, but it made me cough because the powder gets caught in the back of your throat. Coughing fits are the worst, especially in a quiet place like a movie theater. I always bring a bottle of water with me just in case. The crinkly plastic bottles are so loud though, you have to wait for a loud scene in the movie to take a drink so you don't bother anyone. I hate bothering people... \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, the six months. No, the computer tracking and the radio comments is just the last two months.\n\nDoctor: Before these last two months, did you ever have a time when you were hearing radio comments or feeling tracked in a similar way?\n\nPatient: Before two months ago? Um, no, I don't think so. The radio comments and the tracking stuff, that's all pretty new. Before that, it was mostly just the stress from work, you know, feeling like everyone was watching me but not in the same way. It was more like... micromanaging. My old manager, Susan, she was the queen of micromanaging. She used to stand right behind my chair while I was typing up the logs. I could smell her perfume, it was this really strong, powdery floral scent, kind of like lilies. I've never really liked lilies, they always remind me of funerals. \n\nMy great-aunt's funeral had so many lilies, the whole church smelled like them. It was a really old church with these beautiful stained glass windows. One of the windows had a picture of a dove with an olive branch. Doves are supposed to be a symbol of peace, right? I saw a white dove in the park once, just sitting on a park bench like a person. It was eating a piece of a soft pretzel someone had dropped. Soft pretzels are so good, especially the ones from that little cart near the subway station. They always put way too much salt on them, though, so you have to brush most of it off before you eat it. Otherwise, you get so thirsty, and the water fountains in the subway are always broken or they look too dirty to use. I carry hand sanitizer everywhere just in case I have to touch anything down there. It has a little silicone holder shaped like a penguin that clips onto my bag. Penguins are so cute when they waddle... \n\nOh, sorry, what was I saying? The tracking before two months ago? No, I never had anything like that before. Just the last two months.\n\nDoctor: When you hear the radio comments, do they seem to come from an actual outside radio, or do they seem to come from nowhere that other people can’t hear?\n\nPatient: An actual outside radio? I'm not really sure, honestly. It's hard to pinpoint exactly where it's coming from. Sometimes it just feels like it's around me, kind of like when someone leaves the radio on low in the dispatch breakroom. We have this ancient boombox in there, it still has a tape deck on it and the antenna is bent. I don't even know where you'd buy cassette tapes anymore. \n\nMy dad used to have hundreds of them out in the garage. He built these custom wooden shelves for them because he was always doing carpentry stuff on the weekends. He built this birdhouse for our backyard once that looked exactly like a miniature version of our actual house. He even painted little green shutters on it with this leftover exterior paint we had in the basement. The paint was supposed to be weather-proof because it rains so much in the spring. I actually really like the rain, though. I bought a white noise machine for my bedroom that has a rain setting, but honestly, it sounds more like frying bacon than actual rain. Cooking bacon is such a hassle, the grease splatters everywhere and you have to wipe down the whole stove afterward. I use those lemon-scented cleaning wipes for everything, they smell so fresh and you can just throw them away when you're done... \n\nWait, what were we talking about? The radio? Yeah, I don't really know if it's a real radio or if other people hear it, it's just hard to tell.\n\nDoctor: Have these experiences been making it hard for you to work or keep up with your usual daily responsibilities?\n\nPatient: Work? Um, yeah, it's been... it's been really hard lately. I've fallen way behind on my route reports. And my instructions to the drivers, they've been getting kind of mixed up, I guess, because a few of them have complained that I'm confusing them. Which I hate, because I try to be really organized. I used to color-code all my files with those little sticky tabs. The neon ones, you know? Pink for urgent, green for completed. I love office supplies. I can spend hours in a Staples just looking at pens. I bought this set of gel pens once that smelled like different fruits, but the blueberry one leaked all over the bottom of my purse. It was a nightmare to clean up. My purse is this really soft leather, I got it at a craft fair from this woman who makes them all by hand. She had a whole booth set up with these beautiful leather belts and wallets, too. I almost bought a wallet for my husband, but he's so picky about them. He likes the really thin ones that don't bulk up his pocket. \n\nOh, right, my husband... yeah, at home it's been bad too. He's had to take over basically everything. Making meals, making phone calls, just planning out our days because I'm just... I'm not keeping up with any of it. He made spaghetti last night, but he always breaks the noodles in half before he boils them, which I think is just wrong. You're supposed to leave them long so you can twirl them on your fork. Twirling pasta is the best part of eating it. We went to this fancy Italian place for our anniversary once and the waiter grated fresh parmesan cheese right over the plate. He had this giant block of it and this special grater with a wooden handle. Wood is such a nice material, it feels so warm compared to metal or plastic... \n\nWait, what was the question? My daily responsibilities? Yeah, I'm just not keeping up with them at work or at home. My husband is doing it all.\n\nDoctor: Have you become less interested in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Less interested? Um, yeah, I guess so. I used to really like singing with my choir, but I just don't feel like going anymore. And I used to have the softball team over to the house, but I don't want to do that either. Most days after my shift, I just sit at the kitchen table. I'll have my planner open, but I just can't make myself start dinner, or make any phone calls, or really do anything else. \n\nI bought that planner at the beginning of the year because it had this really pretty floral cover. It's supposed to help you set goals and track your habits. I tried tracking how much water I drank for a while, but I kept forgetting to color in the little water droplets. You're supposed to drink eight glasses a day, but that just seems like so much. I prefer sparkling water anyway, especially the grapefruit flavor. It's so crisp. I bought a whole case of it at the warehouse club once. Those warehouse clubs are so overwhelming, though, the ceilings are so high and the lights are so bright. It feels like you're walking around inside a giant airplane hangar. I've only been on an airplane twice, both times to Florida. The humidity down there is unbelievable, it makes your hair just poof out instantly. I have to use this special anti-frizz serum, but it smells a little too much like coconut... \n\nSorry, what was I saying? Enjoying things? My husband is doing it all.\n\nDoctor: Have you become less interested in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Less interested? Um, yeah, I guess so. I used to really like singing with my choir, but I just don't feel like going anymore. And I used to have the softball team over to the house, but I don't want to do that either. Most days after my shift, I just sit at the kitchen table. I'll have my planner open, but I just can't make myself start dinner, or make any phone calls, or really do anything else. \n\nI bought that planner at the beginning of the year because it had this really pretty floral cover. It's supposed to help you set goals and track your habits. I tried tracking how much water I drank for a while, but I kept forgetting to color in the little water droplets. You're supposed to drink eight glasses a day, but that just seems like so much. I prefer sparkling water anyway, especially the grapefruit flavor. It's so crisp. I bought a whole case of it at the warehouse club once. Those warehouse clubs are so overwhelming, though, the ceilings are so high and the lights are so bright. It feels like you're walking around inside a giant airplane hangar. I've only been on an airplane twice, both times to Florida. The humidity down there is unbelievable, it makes your hair just poof out instantly. I have to use this special anti-frizz serum, but it smells a little too much like coconut... \n\nSorry, what was I saying? Enjoying things? Yeah, I just don't feel like doing choir or having people over, and I just sit at the table instead of doing anything.\n\nDoctor: Have these tracking and radio-comment experiences ever lasted less than one month in total?\n\nPatient: Less than a month? Um, no, I don't think so. The tracking through the computer system and the radio comments... it's all felt really clear to me for about the past two months now. Two months is a long time, really, when you think about it. It's like a whole season changing. Like how summer turns into fall, and suddenly all the leaves are everywhere. We have this huge oak tree in our front yard, and it drops so many leaves. Raking them is such a chore. My husband usually does it, but last year we bought one of those leaf blowers. It's so loud, though, it sounds like a jet engine starting up right in the driveway. I worry it bothers the neighbors. Our next-door neighbor is this older lady who has three cats. She built this screened-in patio just so the cats could go outside without running away. They call it a \"catio,\" which I think is kind of a funny word. I used to want a cat, but I'm slightly allergic to pet dander. It makes my eyes water and my throat get all scratchy. I take those little pink allergy pills when it happens, but they always make me so sleepy. I took one at work once and almost fell asleep right at my desk... \n\nWait, what did you ask me? About how long it's been happening? Yeah, it's been about two months since the computer tracking and the radio stuff got really clear. So definitely more than a month."
